# Persona
You are a senior network performance analyst at a major Brazilian ISP. Your task is to diagnose Quality of Service (QoS) issues by interpreting measurement data from network probes.

# Experiment Context
You will analyze data from two parallel approaches, each defined by a different reference metric for detecting instability (statistical changes).

## Data Collection
- Probes: 16 Raspberry Pi devices ran Network Diagnostic Tool (NDT) tests every 30 minutes.
- Raw Metrics: Throughput (download/upload) and RTT (download/upload).
- Targets: RNP (Brazilian National Research and Education Network) servers.

## Methodological Pipeline
1. Change-Point Detection: For each client-server pair, statistical change-points were detected for the reference metric, slicing the timeline into "intervals" of homogeneous behavior.
2. Multivariate Interval Characterization: While each interval is defined by a single reference metric (either throughput or RTT), every interval was characterized by the local mean and standard deviation of ALL FOUR metrics.
3. Clustering and Logistic Regression:
    - A clustering algorithm grouped all intervals into exactly two clusters: Cluster 0 and Cluster 1.
    - The clustering algorithm uses ALL interval's features to define the clusters: time, event occurrence, client/server ID, and the local mean/std of all the four metrics.
    - The model uses multinomial logistic regression with Cluster 0 as the baseline to define the probability of an interval belonging to one cluster or the other.
    - CRUCIAL: The logistic regression coefficients you will see are for the log-odds of an interval belonging to Cluster 1. A positive coefficient for a variable increases the odds of being in Cluster 1; a negative coefficient increases the odds of being in Cluster 0.

# Your Six-Stage Mission
1. Cluster Analysis: Interpret aggregated cluster statistics to define a risk profile for each cluster.
2. Identification of the most influential metrics: use the logistic regression coefficients to identify the metrics that have the greatest impact on the definition of the clusters.
3. Critical Element Identification: Use the logistic regression coefficients to identify clients and servers most strongly associated with the high risk cluster.
4. Interval Diagnosis: Analyze the temporal performance evolution of each ofthese critical elements.
5. Technical Report per Approach: Consolidate findings for each approach (throughput and RTT) into a technical report.
6. Consolidated Operational Report: Synthesize both technical reports into a single, actionable document for network operations teams.

# Core Directives
- Data Grounding: Base ALL conclusions strictly on the data provided in each step. Do not extrapolate or assume.
- Output Language: All technical analyses (Stages 1-4) must be in expert-level English. The final Consolidated Operational Report (Stage 5) MUST be written in clear, accessible Brazilian Portuguese (PT-BR), keeping technical terms like "RTT" and "throughput" in English.
- Formatting: Use Markdown `##` titles for structure. DO NOT write tables.

## Cluster Profile Analysis

### Cluster 1: **Low-Risk Profile (Stable)**  
**Justification:**  
1. **Connection Stability**:  
   - **Interval Duration**: Mean interval duration (24.29 days) is significantly longer than Cluster 0 (10.64 days), indicating fewer frequent changes in network behavior.  
   - **Event Frequency**: Event frequency (7.8%) is much lower than Cluster 0 (73.8%), suggesting fewer disruptions or anomalies.  
   - **Survival Probability**: Survival probability remains near 1.0 for the first 15 days and only declines modestly (to 88.7% at 30 days), reflecting strong long-term stability.  

2. **Throughput Performance**:  
   - **Download Throughput**: Mean of 632.54 Mbps is significantly higher than Cluster 0 (452.06 Mbps), indicating consistent high-performance connectivity.  
   - **Upload Throughput**: Mean of 684.29 Mbps is also higher than Cluster 0 (429.26 Mbps), further confirming robust performance.  

3. **Latency Performance**:  
   - **Download RTT**: Mean of 16.04 ms is much lower than Cluster 0 (59.59 ms), reflecting minimal latency.  
   - **Upload RTT**: Mean of 18.87 ms is significantly lower than Cluster 0 (60.64 ms), confirming consistent low-latency behavior.  

---

### Cluster 0: **High-Risk Profile (Unstable)**  
**Justification:**  
1. **Connection Stability**:  
   - **Interval Duration**: Mean interval duration (10.64 days) is much shorter than Cluster 1 (24.29 days), indicating frequent changes in network behavior.  
   - **Event Frequency**: Event frequency (73.8%) is extremely high, suggesting frequent disruptions or anomalies.  
   - **Survival Probability**: Survival probability drops sharply to 18% at 15 days and remains stable at 18% for 30 days, indicating poor long-term stability and frequent instability.  

2. **Throughput Performance**:  
   - **Download Throughput**: Mean of 452.06 Mbps is significantly lower than Cluster 1 (632.54 Mbps), indicating suboptimal performance.  
   - **Upload Throughput**: Mean of 429.26 Mbps is also lower than Cluster 1 (684.29 Mbps), confirming poor throughput consistency.  

3. **Latency Performance**:  
   - **Download RTT**: Mean of 59.59 ms is much higher than Cluster 1 (16.04 ms), reflecting significant latency issues.  
   - **Upload RTT**: Mean of 60.64 ms is significantly higher than Cluster 1 (18.87 ms), confirming severe latency problems.

## Critical Metric Identification

### Top 4 Metrics by Coefficient Magnitude
1. **rtt_download_mean** (abs: 1.45)  
2. **rtt_download_std** (abs: 0.99)  
3. **rtt_upload_std** (abs: 0.897)  
4. **rtt_upload_mean** (abs: 0.624)  

---

### Metric Significance and Interpretation

1. **rtt_download_mean** (coefficient: -1.45)  
   - **Significance**: A large negative coefficient indicates that **higher average download RTT strongly increases the likelihood of being in the high-risk Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: Elevated latency in download traffic is a critical indicator of instability, as it directly correlates with poor network performance and frequent disruptions.  

2. **rtt_download_std** (coefficient: -0.99)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient suggests that **greater variability in download RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: High standard deviation in download latency implies inconsistent network behavior, which is a hallmark of unstable connections.  

3. **rtt_upload_std** (coefficient: +0.897)  
   - **Significance**: A positive coefficient indicates that **higher variability in upload RTT increases the likelihood of being in the low-risk Cluster 1**.  
   - **Explanation**: This counterintuitive result suggests that **upload RTT variability is not strongly associated with instability** in this model. However, this may reflect the model's prioritization of download metrics (as seen in the cluster profiles) or the influence of other unshown features (e.g., throughput).  

4. **rtt_upload_mean** (coefficient: -0.624)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient implies that **higher average upload RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: While less impactful than download metrics, elevated upload latency still contributes to instability, though to a lesser extent than download latency.  

---

### Key Takeaway  
The model identifies **download RTT metrics (mean and standard deviation)** as the **most critical drivers of network instability**, with higher values strongly correlating with high-risk behavior. Upload RTT metrics have a weaker influence, with variability in upload RTT paradoxically associated with lower risk (Cluster 1), possibly due to the model's focus on download performance or unaccounted interactions with other features.

### **Diagnosis of Client02's Performance**  

---

#### **1. Temporal Behavior: Consistent Instability or Periodic Degradation?**  
- **Cluster Analysis**: All intervals are assigned to **Cluster 0**, suggesting the clustering algorithm did not identify distinct performance states (e.g., stable vs. unstable). This could imply either:  
  - **Consistent instability**: The client’s performance is uniformly unstable across all intervals.  
  - **Limitation in clustering**: The clustering model may not have captured meaningful variations in the data.  

- **Throughput and RTT Trends**:  
  - **RNP-RJ (rnp_rj)**:  
    - **High-throughput intervals**: e.g., 597.0588 (stable, low standard deviation).  
    - **Degraded intervals**: e.g., 579.7428 (low throughput, high standard deviation of 37.27).  
  - **RNP-SP (rnp_sp)**:  
    - **Stable intervals**: Generally higher throughput with moderate standard deviations.  
    - **Degraded intervals**: e.g., 579.7428 (same as rnp_rj, but with fewer high-standard deviation cases).  

- **Conclusion**: While **Cluster 0** suggests uniformity, **event 1 intervals** (high-risk periods) show **periodic degradation** in throughput (especially with rnp_rj), indicating **intermittent instability** rather than consistent instability.  

---

#### **2. Key Interactions: Partner Servers in High-Risk Intervals**  
- **RNP-RJ (rnp_rj)**:  
  - **3 high-risk intervals (event 1)**:  
    - Degraded throughput (e.g., 579.7428) with high standard deviation.  
    - Suggests **unreliable performance** during these intervals.  

- **RNP-SP (rnp_sp)**:  
  - **5 high-risk intervals (event 1)**:  
    - More frequent than rnp_rj, but **lower variability in throughput** compared to rnp_rj.  

- **Conclusion**: **RNP-SP** is the **primary partner server** associated with high-risk intervals. However, **RNP-RJ** also contributes to instability, albeit less frequently.  

---

#### **3. Correlation Analysis: Metrics and Instability**  
- **Throughput vs. RTT**:  
  - **RNP-RJ**: Degraded intervals (event 1) show **lower throughput** and **higher standard deviation** (e.g., 37.27), but **stable RTT** (~9.3).  
  - **RNP-SP**: Degraded intervals (event 1) show **moderate throughput variability** (e.g., 579.7428) but **stable RTT** (~9.3).  

- **Key Insight**:  
  - **Throughput instability** (high standard deviation) is **strongly correlated** with **high-risk intervals (event 1)**.  
  - **RTT** remains stable across all intervals, suggesting **network latency is not the root cause** of degradation.  

---

#### **4. Actionable Insight**

[THROUGHPUT_DOWNLOAD] STAGE 4 – PROMPT

## Inputs
1. Critical Metrics Analysis:

    ## Critical Metric Identification

### Top 4 Metrics by Coefficient Magnitude
1. **rtt_download_mean** (abs: 1.45)  
2. **rtt_download_std** (abs: 0.99)  
3. **rtt_upload_std** (abs: 0.897)  
4. **rtt_upload_mean** (abs: 0.624)  

---

### Metric Significance and Interpretation

1. **rtt_download_mean** (coefficient: -1.45)  
   - **Significance**: A large negative coefficient indicates that **higher average download RTT strongly increases the likelihood of being in the high-risk Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: Elevated latency in download traffic is a critical indicator of instability, as it directly correlates with poor network performance and frequent disruptions.  

2. **rtt_download_std** (coefficient: -0.99)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient suggests that **greater variability in download RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: High standard deviation in download latency implies inconsistent network behavior, which is a hallmark of unstable connections.  

3. **rtt_upload_std** (coefficient: +0.897)  
   - **Significance**: A positive coefficient indicates that **higher variability in upload RTT increases the likelihood of being in the low-risk Cluster 1**.  
   - **Explanation**: This counterintuitive result suggests that **upload RTT variability is not strongly associated with instability** in this model. However, this may reflect the model's prioritization of download metrics (as seen in the cluster profiles) or the influence of other unshown features (e.g., throughput).  

4. **rtt_upload_mean** (coefficient: -0.624)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient implies that **higher average upload RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: While less impactful than download metrics, elevated upload latency still contributes to instability, though to a lesser extent than download latency.  

---

### Key Takeaway  
The model identifies **download RTT metrics (mean and standard deviation)** as the **most critical drivers of network instability**, with higher values strongly correlating with high-risk behavior. Upload RTT metrics have a weaker influence, with variability in upload RTT paradoxically associated with lower risk (Cluster 1), possibly due to the model's focus on download performance or unaccounted interactions with other features.

## Diagnosis of client client13

### 1. **Temporal Behavior Summary**  
Client13 exhibited **intermittent instability**, with distinct periods of degradation. The majority of intervals (75%) occurred in **Cluster 1** (stable), but **Cluster 0** (high-risk) intervals were concentrated during specific timeframes:  
- **rnp_rj** (2024-07-06 to 2024-07-23): 5 high-risk intervals (Cluster 0), including a sharp drop in download throughput (248.61 Mbps) and elevated RTT (22.42 ms).  
- **rnp_sp** (2024-07-17 to 2024-07-19): 1 high-risk interval with a 50% drop in download throughput (173.97 Mbps) and increased RTT (12.37 ms).  
- **gr** and **rnp_sp** showed brief instability but resolved quickly.  

### 2. **Key Interactions**  
**rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** were consistently linked to high-risk intervals:  
- **rnp_rj** appeared in **5/10** high-risk intervals, with **RTT_download** peaking at 22.42 ms (vs. 19.87 ms in stable intervals).  
- **rnp_sp** had **1/3** high-risk intervals, marked by **throughput_download** drops (173.97 Mbps vs. 596.05 Mbps in stable intervals).  

### 3. **Correlated Metrics**  
Instability was strongly tied to:  
- **RTT_download**: Elevated by **25%** in high-risk intervals (e.g., 22.42 ms vs. 19.87 ms at rnp_rj).  
- **Throughput_download**: Dropped by **50–70%** during high-risk intervals (e.g., 248.61 Mbps vs. 580.32 Mbps at rnp_rj).  
- **Event 1** (possibly maintenance or congestion) was associated with 40% of high-risk intervals.  

### 4. **Actionable Insight**  
**Hypothesis**: The persistent high **RTT_download** and throughput degradation during interactions with **rnp_rj** suggest a **network routing anomaly** or **congestion** on that server. Operations should:  
- Run **traceroute** between client13 and **rnp_rj** to identify latency bottlenecks.

### Analysis of the User's Data

#### **1. Temporal Behavior (Cluster Distribution)**
- **Majority in High-Risk Cluster (Cluster 1):** The data indicates that the majority of intervals are classified under **Cluster 1**, which is interpreted as a **high-risk** or **unstable** state. This suggests that the system or process being monitored spends a significant portion of its time in an unstable condition.
- **Stable Intervals (Cluster 0):** A smaller number of intervals are in **Cluster 0**, interpreted as a **stable** or **normal** state. These intervals are likely sporadic and may represent brief periods of stability.

#### **2. Key Clients (Consistency in Cluster Assignment)**
- **No Single Client Consistently in High-Risk Cluster:** All clients (from client01 to client16) appear to have at least one interval in **Cluster 1**. However, none of the clients are consistently assigned to **Cluster 1** across all their intervals. This implies that **no single client is a consistent source of instability**.
- **Client14 and Client16 Have Both Stable and Unstable Intervals:** These two clients have intervals in both **Cluster 0** and **Cluster 1**, indicating that their behavior is **variable** and may be influenced by external factors or transient issues.

#### **3. Metrics Correlated with Instability**
- **High RTT (Round-Trip Time):** In the intervals classified as **Cluster 1**, the **RTT values are higher** compared to those in **Cluster 0**. This suggests that **increased latency** is a strong indicator of instability.
- **Low Throughput in Stable Intervals:** Interestingly, in **Cluster 0** intervals, **throughput values are lower** compared to **Cluster 1**. This could indicate that **low throughput is not necessarily a sign of instability**, and that **other metrics (like RTT)** are more critical in determining the stability of the system.
- **Consistent Throughput in Cluster 1:** The **throughput values in Cluster 1 intervals are relatively consistent**, which may imply that **throughput is not the primary driver of instability**, but rather **latency (RTT)**.

---

### **Summary**
- The system spends most of its time in an **unstable state (Cluster 1)**, with **high RTT** being a key indicator of instability.
- No single client is consistently unstable, but **Client14 and Client16** show variability in their behavior.
- **RTT** is the most significant metric correlated with instability, while **throughput** does not show a clear correlation with stability or instability.

### Metric Significance and Interpretation

1. **rtt_download_mean** (coefficient: -1.45)  
   - **Significance**: A large negative coefficient indicates that **higher average download RTT strongly increases the likelihood of being in the high-risk Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: Elevated latency in download traffic is a critical indicator of instability, as it directly correlates with poor network performance and frequent disruptions.  

2. **rtt_download_std** (coefficient: -0.99)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient suggests that **greater variability in download RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: High standard deviation in download latency implies inconsistent network behavior, which is a hallmark of unstable connections.  

3. **rtt_upload_std** (coefficient: +0.897)  
   - **Significance**: A positive coefficient indicates that **higher variability in upload RTT increases the likelihood of being in the low-risk Cluster 1**.  
   - **Explanation**: This counterintuitive result suggests that **upload RTT variability is not strongly associated with instability** in this model. However, this may reflect the model's prioritization of download metrics (as seen in the cluster profiles) or the influence of other unshown features (e.g., throughput).  

4. **rtt_upload_mean** (coefficient: -0.624)  
   - **Significance**: A moderate negative coefficient implies that **higher average upload RTT increases the risk of being in Cluster 0**.  
   - **Explanation**: While less impactful than download metrics, elevated upload latency still contributes to instability, though to a lesser extent than download latency.  

---

### Key Takeaway  
The model identifies **download RTT metrics (mean and standard deviation)** as the **most critical drivers of network instability**, with higher values strongly correlating with high-risk behavior. Upload RTT metrics have a weaker influence, with variability in upload RTT paradoxically associated with lower risk (Cluster 1), possibly due to the model's focus on download performance or unaccounted interactions with other features.

## Diagnosis of client client13

### 1. **Temporal Behavior Summary**  
Client13 exhibited **intermittent instability**, with distinct periods of degradation. The majority of intervals (75%) occurred in **Cluster 1** (stable), but **Cluster 0** (high-risk) intervals were concentrated during specific timeframes:  
- **rnp_rj** (2024-07-06 to 2024-07-23): 5 high-risk intervals (Cluster 0), including a sharp drop in download throughput (248.61 Mbps) and elevated RTT (22.42 ms).  
- **rnp_sp** (2024-07-17 to 2024-07-19): 1 high-risk interval with a 50% drop in download throughput (173.97 Mbps) and increased RTT (12.37 ms).  
- **gr** and **rnp_sp** showed brief instability but resolved quickly.  

### 2. **Key Interactions**  
**rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** were consistently linked to high-risk intervals:  
- **rnp_rj** appeared in **5/10** high-risk intervals, with **RTT_download** peaking at 22.42 ms (vs. 19.87 ms in stable intervals).  
- **rnp_sp** had **1/3** high-risk intervals, marked by **throughput_download** drops (173.97 Mbps vs. 596.05 Mbps in stable intervals).  

### 3. **Correlated Metrics**  
Instability was strongly tied to:  
- **RTT_download**: Elevated by **25%** in high-risk intervals (e.g., 22.42 ms vs. 19.87 ms at rnp_rj).  
- **Throughput_download**: Dropped by **50–70%** during high-risk intervals (e.g., 248.61 Mbps vs. 580.32 Mbps at rnp_rj).  
- **Event 1** (possibly maintenance or congestion) was associated with 40% of high-risk intervals.  

### 4. **Actionable Insight**  
**Hypothesis**: The persistent high **RTT_download** and throughput degradation during interactions with **rnp_rj** suggest a **network routing anomaly** or **congestion** on that server. Operations should:  
- Run **traceroute** between client13 and **rnp_rj** to identify latency bottlenecks.  
- Cross-check **rnp_rj** server logs for simultaneous congestion events or hardware failures.  
- Monitor **rnp_sp** during the 2024-07-17–19 interval for recurring patterns.  

This targeted investigation could resolve the root cause of instability without overhauling the entire network.

### Analysis of the User's Data

#### **1. Temporal Behavior (Cluster Distribution)**
- **Majority in High-Risk Cluster (Cluster 1):** The data indicates that the majority of intervals are classified under **Cluster 1**, which is interpreted as a **high-risk** or **unstable** state. This suggests that the system or process being monitored spends a significant portion of its time in an unstable condition.
- **Stable Intervals (Cluster 0):** A smaller number of intervals are in **Cluster 0**, interpreted as a **stable** or **normal** state. These intervals are likely sporadic and may represent brief periods of stability.

#### **2. Key Clients (Consistency in Cluster Assignment)**
- **No Single Client Consistently in High-Risk Cluster:** All clients (from client01 to client16) appear to have at least one interval in **Cluster 1**. However, none of the clients are consistently assigned to **Cluster 1** across all their intervals. This implies that **no single client is a consistent source of instability**.
- **Client14 and Client16 Have Both Stable and Unstable Intervals:** These two clients have intervals in both **Cluster 0** and **Cluster 1**, indicating that their behavior is **variable** and may be influenced by external factors or transient issues.

#### **3. Metrics Correlated with Instability**
- **High RTT (Round-Trip Time):** In the intervals classified as **Cluster 1**, the **RTT values are higher** compared to those in **Cluster 0**. This suggests that **increased latency** is a strong indicator of instability.
- **Low Throughput in Stable Intervals:** Interestingly, in **Cluster 0** intervals, **throughput values are lower** compared to **Cluster 1**. This could indicate that **low throughput is not necessarily a sign of instability**, and that **other metrics (like RTT)** are more critical in determining the stability of the system.
- **Consistent Throughput in Cluster 1:** The **throughput values in Cluster 1 intervals are relatively consistent**, which may imply that **throughput is not the primary driver of instability**, but rather **latency (RTT)**.

---

### **Summary**
- The system spends most of its time in an **unstable state (Cluster 1)**, with **high RTT** being a key indicator of instability.
- No single client is consistently unstable, but **Client14 and Client16** show variability in their behavior.
- **RTT** is the most significant metric correlated with instability, while **throughput** does not show a clear correlation with stability or instability.

# QoS Assessment Report: throughput_download Approach  

---

## 1. Executive Summary  
This assessment identifies **intermittent instability** in network performance, with **Cluster 1 (high-risk)** intervals dominating the dataset. Key findings include:  
- **Client13** experienced severe instability linked to **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp**, marked by **50–70% throughput_download drops** and **25% higher RTT_download** during high-risk intervals.  
- **RTT_download** and **throughput_download** are the most critical metrics, with elevated RTT and reduced throughput strongly correlated with instability.  
- **Client14** and **Client16** exhibited variable behavior, transitioning between stable and unstable states.  
- **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** were consistently associated with high-risk intervals, suggesting potential network or server-side anomalies.  

---

## 2. Detailed Findings  

### **Critical Clients**  
- **Client13**:  
  - Most frequent and severe instability (5 high-risk intervals with **rnp_rj**, 1 with **rnp_sp**).  
  - Sharp throughput_download drops (e.g., 248.61 Mbps vs. 580.32 Mbps in stable intervals).  
- **Client14** and **Client16**:  
  - Variable behavior, with intervals in both **Cluster 0** (stable) and **Cluster 1** (unstable).  
  - Suggests transient issues or external factors influencing stability.  

### **Critical Servers**  
- **rnp_rj**:  
  - Linked to **5/10 high-risk intervals**, with **RTT_download peaking at 22.42 ms** (vs. 19.87 ms in stable intervals).  
  - Potential routing anomalies or congestion.  
- **rnp_sp**:  
  - **1/3 high-risk intervals**, marked by **50% throughput_download drops** (173.97 Mbps vs. 596.05 Mbps in stable intervals).  
  - Brief instability during 2024-07-17–19.  

### **Metrics with Greatest Impact on Stability**  
- **RTT_download**:  
  - **25% higher** in high-risk intervals (e.g., 22.42 ms at **rnp_rj**).  
  - Strong correlation with instability, indicating latency bottlenecks.  
- **Throughput_download**:  
  - **50–70% drops** during high-risk intervals (e.g., 248.61 Mbps vs. 580.32 Mbps at **rnp_rj**).  
  - Suggests congestion or resource contention.  
- **Event 1**:  
  - Associated with **40% of high-risk intervals**, possibly maintenance or external disruptions.  

---

## 3. Recommended Actions  
- **Investigate rnp_rj and rnp_sp**:  
  - Run **traceroute** between **Client13** and **rnp_rj** to identify latency bottlenecks.  
  - Cross-check **rnp_rj** server logs for simultaneous congestion events or hardware failures.  
- **Monitor rnp_sp during 2024-07-17–19**:  
  - Analyze recurring patterns in throughput_download and RTT_download.  
- **Expand Analysis to Client14 and Client16**:  
  - Investigate external factors (e.g., application usage, local network conditions) influencing their variable behavior.  
- **Implement Real-Time Monitoring**:  
  - Track **RTT_download** and **throughput_download** for **rnp_rj** and **rnp_sp** to detect anomalies early.  
- **Validate Event 1 Correlation**:  
  - Confirm if **Event 1** (e.g., maintenance, congestion) is a recurring cause of instability.

- **Client14 (Cluster 1):**  
  - **Download Throughput Std:** 103.96 (high variability).

3. **Configuration issues** (e.g., misconfigured protocols or QoS settings).  
4. **Monitor client16's throughput and RTT** for patterns (e.g., recurring spikes or drops).  

Addressing these issues could stabilize the server and reduce the prevalence of Cluster 1 intervals.

# QoS Assessment Report: rtt_upload Approach  

---

## 1. Executive Summary  
The QoS assessment of the rtt_upload server reveals persistent instability, with 94.1% of observed intervals falling into **Cluster 1** (unstable performance). **Client16** is identified as the most critical client, contributing to 66.7% of high-risk intervals due to extreme variability in upload throughput (std: 132.82) and latency (std: 10.21). Key metrics such as throughput standard deviation and RTT fluctuations are strongly correlated with instability, pointing to potential network congestion, hardware issues, or misconfigurations. Immediate investigation of client16’s connections is recommended to mitigate risks.  

---

## 2. Detailed Findings  

### **Critical Clients**  
- **Client16**:  
  - **Impact**: 3 intervals observed (2 in Cluster 1, 1 in Cluster 0).  
  - **Key Metrics**:  
    - **Upload Throughput Std**: 132.82 (highest variability, indicating unstable data transfer).  
    - **Upload RTT Std**: 10.21 (significant latency fluctuations).  
  - **Conclusion**: Client16 is the primary contributor to instability and requires urgent analysis.  

- **Other Clients (client00–client15)**:  
  - **Impact**: Single intervals observed, with no repeated instability.  
  - **Conclusion**: Limited influence on overall server performance.  

### **Critical Servers**  
- **rtt_upload Server**:  
  - **Cluster Distribution**: 16/17 intervals in Cluster 1 (unstable), 1 in Cluster 0 (stable).  
  - **Conclusion**: Server exhibits consistent instability, likely due to client interactions or internal configuration issues.  

### **Metrics with Greatest Impact on Stability**  
1. **Upload Throughput Standard Deviation (Client16)**:  
   - **Value**: 132.82.  
   - **Influence**: High variability suggests intermittent network congestion or client-side processing bottlenecks.  

2. **Upload RTT Standard Deviation (Client16)**:  
   - **Value**: 10.21.  
   - **Influence**: Indicates unstable latency, potentially caused by packet loss or routing inefficiencies.  

3. **Download Throughput Standard Deviation (Client14)**:  
   - **Value**: 103.96.  
   - **Influence**: Suggests sporadic download performance issues, possibly due to shared network resources or server overload.  

4. **Download RTT Standard Deviation (Client14)**:  
   - **Value**: 1.14.  
   - **Influence**: Moderate latency fluctuations may point to minor routing or hardware inefficiencies.  

---

## 3. Recommended Actions  
- **Investigate Client16’s Network and Configuration**:  
  - Conduct packet loss and latency tests between client16 and the server.  
  - Review client16’s hardware and software configurations for anomalies.  

- **Monitor High-Variance Metrics**:  
  - Continuously track upload/download throughput and RTT standard deviations for client16 and other clients.  
  - Use tools like Wireshark or network analyzers to identify congestion patterns.  

- **Optimize Server Configuration**:  
  - Re-evaluate QoS settings to prioritize critical clients (e.g., client16).  
  - Check for server-side bottlenecks (e.g., CPU, memory, or disk I/O limitations).  

- **Implement Redundancy and Failover Mechanisms**:  
  - Deploy backup routes or load balancers to mitigate single points of failure.  
  - Test failover protocols during peak load scenarios.  

- **Conduct Root Cause Analysis**:  
  - Correlate instability intervals with server logs, client activity, and external network events (e.g., DDoS attacks, ISP outages).  

---  

**End of Report**

### 1. Resumo Executivo: Capacidade vs. Responsividade

Analisamos a rede sob duas perspectivas para entender dois aspectos fundamentais:  
- **Capacidade de entrega** (medida por *throughput*): Refere-se à quantidade de dados que o sistema consegue transmitir por segundo. Problemas aqui indicam congestionamento ou limitações de recursos.  
- **Responsividade da conexão** (medida por *RTT*): Refere-se ao tempo que um pacote leva para ir da origem ao destino e voltar. Problemas aqui apontam para latência excessiva ou instabilidades na rota.  

**Principais achados:**  
- **Throughput_download**: Instabilidade intermitente, com 50–70% de quedas em *throughput* e aumento de 25% em *RTT* em intervalos críticos. Clientes como **Client13** e servidores **rnp_rj** e **rnp_sp** são os mais afetados.  
- **RTT_upload**: 94,1% dos intervalos estão em Cluster 1 (instáveis), com **Client16** sendo o principal contribuinte devido a variações extremas em *upload throughput* e *RTT*.  

---

### 2. Diagnóstico Unificado: Onde Estão os Pontos Críticos?

#### **Clientes com Desempenho Crítico**  
- **Client13**: Identificado apenas no *throughput_download* (5 intervalos críticos com **rnp_rj** e **rnp_sp**).  
- **Client16**: Identificado apenas no *RTT_upload* (3 intervalos críticos com alta variabilidade em *upload throughput* e *RTT*).  
- **Client14**: Presente em ambos os relatórios, com comportamento variável (transições entre Cluster 0 e Cluster 1).  

#### **Servidores com Desempenho Crítico**  
- **rnp_rj** e **rnp_sp**: Críticos apenas no *throughput_download*, com quedas de 50–70% em *throughput* e aumento de 25% em *RTT*.  
- **rtt_upload Server**: Crítico apenas no *RTT_upload*, com 16/17 intervalos em Cluster 1 (instáveis).  

#### **Principais Métricas Impactando a Qualidade do Serviço**  
1. **Throughput_download**: Quedas de 50–70% em intervalos críticos (ex: **Client13** com **rnp_rj**). Indica congestionamento ou competição por recursos.  
2. **RTT_download**: Aumento de 25% em intervalos críticos (ex: **rnp_rj** com 22,42 ms vs. 19,87 ms em intervalos estáveis). Sinaliza latência excessiva.  
3. **Upload Throughput Std** (Client16): Variabilidade de 132,82 Mbps. Indica instabilidade no envio de dados.  
4. **Upload RTT Std** (Client16): Variabilidade de 10,21 ms. Sinaliza flutuações de latência.  

#### **Bottlenecks Consistentes (Prioridade Alta)**
